Discussion and future work

The findings of this study highlight that model selection, forecast horizon, and inter-pollutant interactions all significantly impact air-quality predictions across stations and pollutants. Among all tested approaches, the Transformer consistently achieved best performance, underpinning its capacity to represent short-term and long-term trends through its attention mechanism. As a result, it provided a more stable and reliable solution than traditional ML and DL architectures such as CNN and LSTMs, which revealed greater variability and higher cumulative errors at longer horizons. Despite its strong predictive performance, the Transformer model presents inherent interpretability limitations. Although the attention mechanism enables the learning of complex short- and long-term temporal dependencies, attention weights do not constitute direct explanations of causal relationships or pollutant drivers. In policy-relevant air-quality applications, this lack of transparency may constrain the model’s usefulness for regulatory decision-making, as attention distributions can vary across heads and layers and are sensitive to input representations. Consequently, the Transformer in this study should be viewed primarily as a high-accuracy forecasting tool rather than an inherently explainable model.Fig. 9Daily \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} forecasting at Station 3: Transformer model without \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} correlation (top) versus Transformer model with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} correlation (bottom), showing performance improvement with correlated input.

Daily \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} forecasting at Station 3: Transformer model without \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} correlation (top) versus Transformer model with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} correlation (bottom), showing performance improvement with correlated input.

The use of temporal cross-validation revealed important insights into model stability. Models with lower standard deviations across folds like Transformer demonstrated more consistent performance regardless of the temporal period, whereas models with higher variability showed sensitivity to seasonal patterns or data distribution shifts. This uncertainty quantification strengthens confidence in our model rankings and avoids overfitting to a single train-test split.

Short-term forecasting (1–2 hours) further highlighted the strengths of deep learning methods, especially CNN, that often delivered performances comparable to the Transformer. At longer periods ahead (e.g. 1 week), Prophet emerged second-best, reflecting its strong capability to represent seasonal and trend-driven signals. These complementary strengths present encouraging prospect for hybrid frameworks that integrate the flexibility of Transformers and Prophet’s seasonality modeling with CNN’s localized precision. The analysis of cross-pollutant correlations provided further information to enhance forecast accuracy. The inclusion of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} as an input feature improved \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} predictions, especially at stations with strong negative correlations, such as Stations 2, 5, and 6, while adding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} similarly enhanced \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} estimates. These outcomes underscore the value of multi-pollutant modeling in capturing complex chemical and meteorological interactions. However, improvements were not uniform across all stations: weaker correlations like those recorded at Station 4 showed limited benefits, highlighting the need for station-specific approaches.

The observed variability across stations suggests that area-specific characteristics, like human activities, emission sources and meteorological events, can induce sudden changes or spikes in otherwise regular pollution patterns, thereby making forecasting a challenging task for most models. Future research should address these challenges by exploring strategies that explicitly target the prediction of extreme pollution peaks, such as anomaly-aware training or weighted loss functions. In addition, hybrid and ensemble methods, including the integration of Transformers with Prophet or with CNN, represent a promising avenue for enhancing forecasting over all horizons, as recent studies have shown20. Moreover, spatiotemporal modeling, involving graph-based learning or multi-station modeling, offer further potential by exploiting inter-site space-temporal dependencies and providing a more comprehensive portrayal of pollutant behavior. Expanding beyond \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} to include other pollutants, such as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {SO}_{2}$$\end{document} and CO, would further enhance the scope of predictions in urban environments where pollutant interactions are complex and multi-dimensional. Similarly, the inclusion of meteorological features such as weather data, traffic volumes, and land-use data may provide further insight into station-level variability and support model generalizability. In summary, while the Transformer has been the best-performing model across all horizons, CNN and Prophet delivered competitive performance at short-term and long-term horizons, respectively. Moreover, the improved predictive accuracy achieved by incorporating inter-pollutant interactions highlights the promise of multi-pollutant and spatiotemporal forecasting paradigms.

Conclusion

The performance of a range of forecasting models, including classical machine learning algorithms, deep learning approaches, and state-of-the-art time series techniques, was evaluated for predicting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} concentrations at seven monitoring stations in Abu Dhabi. Model performance was assessed over short-term (1–2 hours), medium-term (1 day), and long-term (1 week) periods using three performance measures: MAE, RMSE, and sMAPE. The findings consistently demonstrated the superiority of the Transformer model, which achieved the lowest error rates across all time horizons and for both pollutants, confirming its strong capacity to capture both local fluctuations and temporal dependencies in air quality data. Specifically, the Transformer achieved average sMAPE values ranging from 0.2354 to 0.2981 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} and 0.2030 to 0.2719 for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} across these horizons.

Despite the Transformer being the most reliable approach, competitive models were also noted. CNN showed sturdy performance at short-term forecasting, where accurate identification of local patterns is particularly critical. Nonetheless, Prophet has demonstrated notable strengths at longer forecasting horizons, owing to its ability to identify seasonal and holiday trends. Furthermore, including inter-pollutant interactions yielded significant improvements in forecasting accuracy, especially at stations with strong negative correlations between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document}. These findings exhibit the advantage of multi-pollutant modeling and station-specific strategies for improving air pollutants forecasting. These improvements were particularly evident in 1-hour \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {NO}_{2}$$\end{document} and 1- to 24-hour \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {O}_{3}$$\end{document} forecasts, with reduced error metrics highlighting the advantage of multi-pollutant modeling and station-specific strategies for improving air pollutants forecasting.

Even with these advances, significant challenges remain, particularly in accurately predicting extreme pollution events and accounting for source variability at monitoring stations. Mitigating these limitations through hybrid modeling, spatiotemporal schemes, and the integration of auxiliary data sources remain a promising direction for further research. Overall, this study demonstrates the potential of Transformer-based models as state-of-the-art solution for urban air quality prediction and underscores the advantages of integrating pollutant interactions and complementary modeling techniques. This research provides a basis for future efforts towards designing more accurate, and operationally viable prediction systems to support air quality management and protect public health in Abu Dhabi and beyond.
